```python
class PreparedPublication(BoundaryModel):
    attempt_id: str
    operation_id: str
    input_digest: str
    prepared_digest: str
    inspection_digest: str
    repo: str
    branch: str
    commit: str
    base_commit: str
    destination_digest: str
    changed_paths: tuple[str, ...]
    diff_stat: str

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PrincipalRef:
    provider_instance: str
    stable_id: str
    kind: Literal["app", "bot_user", "service_user", "user", "local_service"]
    display_name: str

class CredentialRequest(BoundaryModel):
    binding: str
    binding_revision: int
    credential_mode: CredentialMode
    repo: str
    purpose: Literal["checkout", "publish", "api"]
    attempt_id: str | None
    operation_id: str | None
    prepared_digest: str | None
    destination_digest: str | None

class CredentialRights(BoundaryModel):
    resource_scope: Literal["repository", "repository_set", "account", "unknown"]
    native_permissions: tuple[str, ...]
    operations: frozenset[Literal["read", "write_refs", "change_requests", "comments"]]

class IssuedCredential(BoundaryModel):
    binding: str
    binding_revision: int
    credential_mode: CredentialMode
    value: SecretValue | None
    principal: PrincipalRef
    rights: CredentialRights | None
    expires_at: datetime | None
    replacement: Literal["reissue", "rotate", "static", "not_applicable"]
    revocation: Literal["native", "expiry_only", "manual", "not_applicable"]

class Vcs(Protocol):
    name: str
    config_schema: JsonObject
    def capabilities(self) -> VcsCapabilities: ...
    async def acquire_credential(self, request: CredentialRequest, ctx: ProtocolContext) -> IssuedCredential: ...
    async def checkout_plan(self, request: CheckoutRequest, ctx: ProtocolContext) -> CheckoutPlan: ...
    async def publish_plan(self, request: PublishRequest, ctx: ProtocolContext) -> PublishPlan: ...
    async def open_change_request(self, ref: VcsRef, title: str, body: str) -> VcsRef: ...
    async def change_request_status(self, ref: VcsRef) -> ChangeRequestStatus: ...
    async def head_sha(self, ref: VcsRef, ctx: ProtocolContext) -> str: ...
    async def post_comment(self, ref: VcsRef, body: str) -> str: ...
    async def ci_status(self, ref: VcsRef) -> CiStatus: ...
    async def request_reviewers(self, ref: VcsRef, recipients: tuple[str, ...]) -> ReviewerRequestOutcome: ...
```

**First-slice activation.** [First-slice.md](first-slice.md) determines installed support: nonempty node hooks/skills/subagents/MCP servers and `trust_repo_config: true` are refused, not passed through or merely warned. Future harness mapping rows below activate with their implementation. Real hook interception of built-in subagent/helper/tool behavior and controlled conformance fixtures remains required for both harnesses.

**Pending models (issue #134).** Three models get their fields with the first slice that needs them: `Feedback`, with the first slice that has a `fresh_with_feedback` retry or a `vcs` approval channel, whose `comments` reuse `FeedbackComment` of section 5; `ChangeRequestStatus` and `CiStatus`, with the first slice that has a code host or a `wait_event` sensor. `ChangeRequestStatus` keeps a `status` field with a `merged` value, which [ir.md](ir.md) and `examples/dependency-upgrade.ir.yaml` already read. The slice-1 git provider supports `head_sha` and declares `change_requests`, `review_comments`, `ci_status`, `head_sha_watch` and `reviewer_requests` false; capability checks refuse the unsupported operations before the call. The slice-1 `Vcs` protocol class leaves out `open_change_request`, `change_request_status`, `post_comment`, `ci_status` and `request_reviewers` until the first slice with a code host, so strict typing needs no placeholder; `HarnessAdapter.build_session_spec` receives `feedback=None` in slice 1 (section 3).

**Workspace values (issue #236).** `WorkspaceRef` and `WorkspaceTarget` check only the bounds of section 3: a root need not be absolute or normalized, and `commit`, `base_commit` and `branch` are protocol identifiers that may start with `-`. Their producers are the runtime and the VCS provider, not the candidate, and a consumer must not assume more. Requiring an absolute normalized root, checking commit and ref forms, or passing `--end-of-options` to every VCS command is decided with the VCS-bearing part of work item 3.

**Protocol models (issue #262).** The section 4 models are closed `BoundaryModel`s, except `VcsCapabilities`, `CheckoutRequest` and `PublishRequest`, frozen dataclasses that pass only between the worker and its provider in process; `PrincipalRef` is the `VcsRef` contract's own. `Vcs` takes the caller's `ProtocolContext` last, as section 1 requires. These rules complete the declarations:

| field | rule |
|---|---|
| `PlanFile.path` | the `VcsRef` relative-path form, at most 4,096 bytes and 255 per component, unique per area within a plan |
| `PlanFile.content`, `PlanStep.stdin` | at most 256 KiB and 64 KiB; written in JSON as padded URL-safe base64 (RFC 4648 section 5) |
| one plan | file content, `stdin`, `argv` and `env` names and values together at most 512 KiB |
| `PlanFile.mode` | 0 to 0o777 |
| `files`, `steps` | at most 64 each; the steps of all phases of a plan count together |
| `PlanStep.argv` | 1 to 256 arguments without NUL, each at most 4 KiB and 64 KiB together |
| `PlanStep.env`, `CheckoutPlan.credentials` | at most 256 entries with `EnvName` keys |
| `PlanStep.timeout` | positive and at most one hour; the step is also bounded by its deadline |
| `PlanStep.capture` | `commit`, `branch`, `changed_paths`, `diff`, `diff_stat`, `inspection` or null |
| `PlanStep.failure_class` | `artifact_invalid`, `config_error`, `infrastructure` or null, and null when omitted; the class the executor reports when the step exits non-zero (#159) |
| `required_tools`, `native_permissions` | at most 64 bounded identifiers |
| `repo`, `branch` | printable text of at most 4,096 and 1,024 bytes |
| `diff_stat` | text without NUL of at most 4,096 bytes |
| `PublishRequest.commit_message` | non-empty text without NUL, at most 64 KiB |
| `CheckoutRequest.rev` | printable text of at most 1,024 bytes, or a `VcsRef` |

- **Plans.** `prepare`, `inspect` and `publish` steps refuse `cwd="candidate"`, and `export` steps require it, because export runs in the candidate domain. The placeholder allow-list above is the executor's check (#159), not the model's.
- **Inspection.** `InspectionContent.path` is null exactly for `revision_metadata`. `InspectionManifest.complete` is a strict boolean that accepts `false`; a false completeness is the scanner's `artifact_invalid` refusal, not a validation error.
- **Prepared publication.** The fields copied into a `VcsRef` keep its forms; `commit` and `base_commit` are at most 255 characters, the changed paths are unique, at most 10,000 and 512 KiB together, and the projected `VcsRef` with a worst-case principal stays within its 1 MiB canonical limit, so a `PreparedPublication` that validates always yields a valid `VcsRef`. The digests carry the `sha256:` prefix.
- **Credentials.** `Binding` is non-empty printable UTF-8 text of at most 256 bytes; text that is not valid UTF-8 is refused before it is measured. `BindingRevision` is an exact integer from 1 to 2^53−1. A `publish` request carries the attempt, operation and both digests; a `checkout` request carries the attempt and no digest; an `api` request carries no digest and an optional attempt, and the slice-1 provider refuses it. In `local_credential_free` mode an `IssuedCredential` has no value, rights or expiry, `not_applicable` replacement and revocation and a `local_service` principal; in `credential_required` mode it has a value, rights, other alternatives and another principal kind. A provider builds a credential-bearing `IssuedCredential` by validating it with its context's `SecretAdmission` under the transfer scope key (section 2). Whether a returned tuple matches its request is the caller's check (#159). The worker route body of `POST /attempts/{att}/credentials` reuses these types.

### Credential-free local publication

`CredentialRequest`, `IssuedCredential` and `PublicationAuthorization` carry the same required `binding`, `binding_revision` and `credential_mode`, without defaults. The binding is an immutable trusted identifier, not a mutable alias: nonempty, at most 256 UTF-8 bytes. The revision is an exact positive JSON integer at most 2^53−1, never a boolean. `CredentialMode` is exactly `credential_required` or `local_credential_free`; it describes credential presence independently of backend kind. Configuration changes advance the revision; different configuration under the same identity/revision is invalid.

The sealed execution request and trusted preparation checkpoint pin the expected binding tuple before preparation. Late authorization cannot initialize or replace it. Acquisition uses that saved tuple, repository and purpose; the authority verifies them against its authorized binding snapshot before resolving secrets or calling an issuer. A mismatch is a content-free `config_error`, with no alias fallback. The returned `IssuedCredential` tuple must exactly match the request. The worker/API rechecks current binding authorization after acquisition through the explicit heartbeat expectation/echo with the lease and desired-state gate, and the executor compares all three authorization fields with the checkpoint independently of the destination digest. These fields do not by themselves provide an instantaneous distributed revocation fence.

The saved trusted binding declares `local_credential_free` mode and a stable local principal. Required fields `PublicationAuthorization.credential: SecretValue | None` and `IssuedCredential.value: SecretValue | None` have no default: explicit null is allowed only by that binding, while omission is malformed. Non-null mode requires a registered SecretValue; a missing/empty secret, resolution failure, local URL or caller flag never selects credential-free mode.

For a credential-free result, require `rights=None` (type becomes CredentialRights | None), `expires_at=None`, `replacement="not_applicable"` and `revocation="not_applicable"`; credential-bearing mode keeps its existing non-null rights and existing enum alternatives. Reject contradictory combinations. Local mode cannot satisfy minimum upstream credential-rights/expiry/revocation requirements, and null expiry never extends attempt authority.

`PrincipalRef.kind="local_service"` identifies a local service. Its `provider_instance` is the trusted local provider authority and `stable_id` an immutable configured service identifier preserved across restart and binding revisions, never a username/UID/path/binding alias/Git author/code-host account. `PublicationAuthorization.principal` embeds the complete trusted principal snapshot from the issued credential or explicit local binding; identity comparison uses `(provider_instance, stable_id)` and needs no separately resolved identifier. Persist the acquired principal with the publication checkpoint before dispatch, retaining the binding identity/revision and mode pinned before preparation. Current aliases or candidate data cannot replace this saved context.

`Vcs.acquire_credential` remains in the first-slice subset: local checkout/publish returns that no-secret result without secret resolution or an issuer; API purpose is unsupported. CheckoutPlan.credentials stays empty. Publish acquisition remains late, after inspection. Adoption reacquires for the original saved binding tuple and operation only; a changed or unavailable binding refuses new publication without rewriting the checkpoint or declaring unknown effects settled. Authorized read-only reconciliation and completed historical replay retain the original destination and provenance. Credential refresh under an unchanged binding remains allowed. The result does not itself authorize dispatch. #156 owns these method/model definitions; #159 owns the local implementation and conformance. A no-secret result is not `vcs.credential_issued`. Publication-related `runtime.phase` records carry scoped principal, trusted binding identity/revision and credential mode, alongside explicit phase/outcome. Worker/API promotion records this append-only audit provenance and mirrors it compactly in attempt.scratch; the runtime owns its authenticated source. An authorization record never claims that a push occurred. Preserve real credential issuance audit semantics.

Allow only absolute POSIX paths or `file:///absolute/path` with empty authority, referring to an existing bare repository. Reject URL userinfo/host/port/query/fragment, relative/scp/helper/network forms, malformed encoding and decoded traversal. Decode URL path exactly once and preserve ordinary escaped filename characters, normalize one filesystem representation and pin/recheck the destination directory identity. Every checkout/head-read/publish/reconcile path consumes that same validated identity. This is a Git transport restriction, not proof that the filesystem is physically local. Reject substitutions through symlinks, parent replacement or changed binding revisions.

Fresh trusted Git metadata/configuration and a constructed environment exclude inherited Git configuration, includes/URL rewrites/pushurl, credential helpers, SSH agents/keys, askpass, candidate hooks/filters and object alternates. Destination-side hooks/configuration are explicitly trusted operator configuration under the cooperative local-runtime model; they must not be described as suppressed by client cleanup. The implementation must test client-side redirection separately, and accepted destination identity remains unchanged.

Local mode uses the existing heartbeat and dispatch gate, not the control-plane credential-issuer route. After inspection and local metadata acquisition, a fresh authenticated heartbeat carrying `PublicationExpectation` for the saved binding tuple, operation and prepared/destination digests precedes each effectful `finalize` or retry. Require the exact response echo of the API's successful saved/current-authority comparison; an ordinary heartbeat or acquisition result cannot substitute. Require `desired_state=run`, execute authority permitting publication, an unexpired lease and unchanged eligible deadline, closed predecessors and no sticky expiry. Serialize response validation and dispatch against closure of the worker-local gate, including closure while awaiting the response; a failed/unavailable check or missing/mismatched echo permits no dispatch. The authorization travels over the authenticated runtime control channel. API cancellation cannot retroactively revoke an already-dispatched push; unknown outcomes retain closure obligations and reservations.

Required owner chain:

- Worker/API: current lease, desired state and binding authorization, authenticated current authority, final check serialized with dispatch.
- Runner/runtime registry: successful inspection, immutable prepared checkpoint, current dispatcher, earlier dispatcher/publication closure, no retirement, serialization/permit.
- Executor: matching attempt/domain/dispatcher/operation/prepared/destination fields, exact saved binding identity/revision/mode, principal/value combination and local deadline before push.
- Worker/API recovery: recorded principal and settled result; unknown publication retains recovery obligations and reservations.

`PublishPermit` remains internal and cannot replace PublicationAuthorization. Match failures use `publication_authorization_mismatch` before publication. `test_publication_authorization_is_late`, `test_publication_destination_is_trusted` and #159's credential/principal test cover explicit-null/omitted-field, binding/principal/mode mismatch, pre-inspection, stale/cancelled/expired/retired authority, unresolved predecessor, adoption, response-loss and no-overwrite cases. `test_protocol_models_are_closed` rejects missing/invalid binding tuple fields and contradictory null combinations. The authorization tests change the binding revision between preparation/acquisition and acquisition/dispatch while keeping principal and destination unchanged; acquisition mismatches resolve no secret and call no issuer. `test_finalize_recovers_prepared_revision` and `test_publish_reconciles_after_response_loss` cover unchanged-binding adoption, changed-binding refusal and retained uncertainty. The provenance retention test preserves the original tuple and rejects conflicting replay on both databases. Only actual remote issuance/rotation/expiry/credential re-scan cases wait for the code-host trigger.

### Shared preparation and publication rules

**Trusted roots and captures.** The runtime privately allocates `control_root` and `publication_root` outside `WorkspaceTarget.root`; `host_path` exposes only the candidate workspace for compatible preparation, never permission to publish there. `PlanFile.path` is relative to its named private area, rejects traversal/absolute paths, and is created with descriptor-relative no-follow operations. Parent replacement, symlinks and writable hard-link aliases cannot redirect private writes. Plans are immutable trusted provider output, not files loaded from the repository. Allowed placeholders in argv/env/stdin are `{candidate_root}`, `{control_root}`, `{publication_root}`, `{input_location}`, `{base_commit}`, `{commit}` and `{branch}`; all values come from supervisor state. There is no shell interpolation.

Captures are bounded typed outputs: `commit` and `branch` are validated single identifiers; `changed_paths` is a JSON array of relative paths, not newline-split text; `diff` is display text, not an executable validation source; `diff_stat` is display text. The provider-owned native decoder additionally emits the common `InspectionManifest` and `InspectionContent` index defined below; an opaque native manifest alone is not sufficient. Missing/malformed captures fail the phase. Core treats `PublicationInput.format` and revision strings as opaque; only the provider understands them. Locations name immutable bytes reachable through the runtime's transfer channel, not a worker-local filesystem assumption.

**Credentials and principals ([ADR-0023](../../docs/decisions/0023-code-host-identity-and-purpose-scoped-credentials.md)).** Only the worker, its private worker-host executor and control-plane providers call `acquire_credential`, never code in the candidate domain. `checkout` is acquired immediately before preparation, `publish` only at late authorization (section 2) with the operation, prepared digest and destination digest set, and `api` per code-host call. `CheckoutPlan.credentials` and the publication steps name environment variables whose values come from the acquired credential of their purpose. The reference GitHub App backend issues in the control plane: the App signing key stays with `kora api`, and the worker requests each credential through the worker-facing issuance route bound to the attempt, its current lease epoch, the purpose and, for `publish`, the bound digests ([api-and-agent-surface.md](api-and-agent-surface.md) section 2). Other backends declare where their parent credential lives.

For credential-bearing modes, `IssuedCredential` reports what the backend enforces upstream: `rights.resource_scope`, the provider's own `native_permissions` with the mapped coarse `operations`, `expires_at` (null without native expiry), `replacement` and `revocation`, each independent of the others. A deployment binding declares the minimum `resource_scope` and `operations` it requires per purpose; a backend that cannot meet them fails acquisition with `config_error`, and nothing falls back to a broader credential. Reports state residual authority: a repository-scoped write token is not a single-push or single-branch grant. `expires_at` is independent of `replacement`: a static credential can still expire, and acquisition of a static credential past its `expires_at` fails with `config_error` instead of returning it. A credential that expires or is rejected is reacquired for the same request; an adopting worker reacquires instead of reusing a predecessor's credential. Values are redacted and never persisted; each issuance writes a `vcs.credential_issued` event with purpose, principal, rights, expiry and binding revision, and no value.

For code-host kinds, `PrincipalRef.stable_id` is the host's immutable account or app identifier, never a login, token id or alias. The `local_service` identity follows the explicit local-mode contract above. Each publication records the actual pushing principal; it records an opening principal only when a change request was actually opened. Local bare-Git branch-only output preserves the absence of opener and host-link fields, including through serialization and recovery. Each binding revision keeps the set of automation principals kora has used for it; a token replacement or a change of bot principal adds to the set. A backend declares whether its rotation preserves the principal.

Reference backends in MVP:

| backend | issuer | reports |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub App | control plane, from the App key | per-attempt installation tokens narrowed to the repository and the purpose's permissions; `expires_at` one hour after issue; `replacement: reissue`; `revocation: native`; principal kind `app` |
| static service credential | the secrets provider | `replacement: static`, `revocation: manual`; `expires_at` as configured by the operator or discovered from the host (for example a personal access token with an expiry date), null only when the credential has no native expiry; rights as configured by the operator, `resource_scope: unknown` unless the operator declares it |
| GitLab project or group token | the secrets provider | the token's scope names in `native_permissions`; date-based `expires_at` when configured; `replacement: rotate` or `static`; principal kind `bot_user` with the token's user id |

Git transport uses the Git CLI with credential-helper and SSH mechanisms; an SSH agent socket or key is a credential and never enters the candidate domain. A non-Git VCS supplies its own plans and backends.

**Preparation.** The selected executor creates an empty candidate root, materializes plan helpers only in control storage, checks required tools and executes the checkout plan with a minimal environment and checkout credentials. It captures `commit`, optional `branch`, and `changed_paths` for a `VcsRef` checkout. The supervisor constructs `WorkspaceRef` using the trusted request and persists it before starting the harness. For a `VcsRef`, `base_commit` comes from that input and protected-path checks run before a command gate. Checkout secrets/helpers and credential stores must not be inherited by candidate code. Reclaiming an attempt adopts a completed preparation rather than deleting a live workspace; a pre-submit absence alone permits no preparation anew while an earlier dispatcher may resume. Recovery observes/adopts the same attempt or retires it under section 2; only a never-dispatched first claim can prepare without resolving earlier authority.

**Export and sealing.** `PublishPlan.export` runs, if needed, in the candidate domain after the session, with no checkout, publication or code-host API credential. Its output is untrusted data until validated. The supervisor then terminates that entire domain and copies the export and selected workspace content into a private `PublicationInput`. Reads must resist path replacement, traversal, symlink dereference and hard-link aliases; symlinks that are legitimate VCS data remain data, not paths the publisher follows. Reject special files or unsupported encodings rather than guessing. Copied bytes have no writable alias in candidate storage. The supervisor fixes their digest before acknowledging sealing. Local uses the same copy/validation path without claiming that a hostile same-user process cannot interfere.

**Prepare and inspect publication.** `prepare` and `inspect` execute only in fresh publication storage, without VCS credentials, candidate configuration, executable attributes, helpers, plugins or live candidate processes. `prepare` yields one exact native revision; it is persisted once with the input and plan digests. `inspect` derives changed paths, review diff and the complete newly published content/history from that revision. kora checks protected paths, scans all newly published content for resolved secret values and common key patterns, and verifies a nonempty change against the trusted base. A hit is `secret_leak`; malformed/empty/unsupported content is `artifact_invalid`. Relevant secret values are available only to the trusted scanner and redactor, not the provider's subprocess environment; the publish credential acquired later is also checked against prepared content before it is delivered to a publish step. Success emits `PreparedPublication` and `awaiting_authorization` from the supervisor. `prepared_digest` binds the native prepared data and its metadata; `destination_digest` binds provider identity, trusted remote, repository, branch, base and transport settings, not just candidate display strings. `operation_id` is deterministic per attempt/publication output. The existing per-attempt publication plan produces the workspace revision; declared outputs referring to that workspace use the same prepared operation rather than allowing different destinations under one identity.

**Inspection handoff to the scanner.** The provider-owned decoder runs as an inspect step in private publication storage, with no scan secret values in its environment. Its required `inspection` capture is an `InspectionManifest`: the exact native revision and prepared digest, a content-index location/digest, and `complete=true`. The content index is a bounded JSON-lines stream of `InspectionContent` records. Each record identifies an opaque native revision, a relative path (or no path for revision metadata), kind, immutable content location, digest and byte length. It enumerates every file/symlink version and revision metadata newly reachable from the trusted base through the prepared revision, including intermediate versions later removed. A non-Git system with snapshot-only publication enumerates that complete new snapshot and its metadata. Native decoding/reachability stays provider-owned; core reads only this common interchange.

The trusted scanner pulls raw bytes through the authenticated private transfer channel, with backpressure, checks index/content digests and sizes against the manifest and binds its verdict to both prepared and inspection digests. It does not run the decoder with secret values or scan a display diff. Provider configuration supplies finite index/count/content-byte and inspect-time bounds, checked at startup; exceeding them is failure, not truncation. Malformed records, missing blobs, changed digests, unsupported kinds, a missing terminal completeness assertion or an incomplete enumeration fail `artifact_invalid` before authorization. Transfer unavailability is infrastructure failure, never successful inspection. Provider conformance verifies native reachability/completeness against real published history, not just a boolean assertion. The checkpoint retains this immutable index/content and verdict for recovery and for checking a newly acquired publication secret before delivering it to the publishing process.

**Finalize.** Publication steps execute only against the prepared revision and trusted destination, with a late credential supplied through section 2's authorization. No inspect/publish step may have `cwd=candidate`; only export and initial checkout may execute there. The authorization is kept out of argv, URLs, journal and persisted plan files; an ephemeral private helper or descriptor passes it only to the publication process. Output is redacted. The resulting `VcsRef` is assembled from trusted request/base, the prepared revision and verified remote result, not mutable workspace captures. `open_change_request` preserves every recorded runtime field/value and adds only absent, verified `url`/`opened_by` and `commit_url`/`compare_url` under the [enrichment and durable recovery contract](artifacts/README.md#5-upload-replay-and-result-ownership). Before create dispatch, persist attempt/output/runtime/destination identity and the acquired full API principal; reconcile actual creation/opener, retain learned effects even when artifact validation fails, and durably freeze the validated candidate before first upload; this remains a code-host API call made by the worker and idempotent on head/base by search before create. Its dispatch participates in the worker gate and must settle or be authoritatively fenced before that dispatcher acknowledges closure; a successful runtime retirement alone cannot settle it. Issuance backend design remains #43.

Local bare-Git publication never calls it: its branch-only `VcsRef` retains required `pushed_by` as the complete scoped `local_service` principal and omits all four fields, never substituting nulls or invented links ([IR artifact contract](ir.md#11-worked-examples)).

**Placement.** Both protocols declare `plan_executors`; prefer `runner`, otherwise `worker_host`. No common executor fails validation with `IR009` and fails execution with `config_error`. The names describe trusted executor placement, not permission to run a publisher inside the candidate domain.

| executor | preparation | publication | capability requirement |
|---|---|---|---|
| `runner` | supervisor in the runtime's execution infrastructure | separate trusted publication environment coordinated by `Runtime.finalize` | installed plan tools and code-host connectivity in the corresponding trusted phases; protected authority when required |
| `worker_host` | common host executor before candidate launch where `host_path` is exposed | runtime adapter transfers sealed bytes to a private worker-host publisher; same `finalize` contract | host client/tools and durable private checkpoint; no credentialed access to the candidate root |

Git declares both placements; local declares both, Docker and the configured fake runtime declare `runner` in MVP. An independent provider may expose no host path and still support complete preparation, sealed transfer, inspection and publication. The VCS sees no runtime name. The runtime adapter coordinates the common executor; it does not implement Git or another VCS.

**Git reference behaviour.** Provider `remotes` map logical repositories to trusted transport settings. Checkout uses a new repository and an explicit requested revision/branch; helpers and an empty hooks directory are private control files, never `.kora/plan/`. The agent may commit locally; kora performs publication. A candidate-side export produces a Git object bundle for the selected HEAD plus a raw selected worktree manifest: all tracked paths and untracked non-ignored paths, excluding candidate `.kora/` and `.git/`. Submodule gitlinks are data; no submodule command is executed. LFS publication is rejected as unsupported in MVP rather than executing a candidate filter or silently omitting required objects.

After sealing, the publisher imports validated, size-bounded objects into a fresh bare repository using strict object checks, with no inherited Git config/environment, alternates, object directories, hooks, fsmonitor, external diff, textconv, credential helper or candidate attributes. Validate that the selected HEAD descends from the recorded checkout commit and that the trusted base is present; never fetch candidate-selected prerequisites. Imported existing commit objects are retained unchanged. A separate trusted index uses raw bytes (`hash-object --no-filters` and index entries), modes and symlink blobs from the manifest to create the final tree, without `git add` or checkout in the candidate repository. If the final tree differs from HEAD, prepare exactly one additional commit with that HEAD as parent and message `kora: <node_path> #<n>`; persist its complete bytes and object id before inspection. Clean HEAD needs no extra commit. The trusted commit identity for that additional commit comes from provider configuration; existing candidate author fields are preserved and do not substitute for the authenticated publishing principal.

Checks read raw blobs and newly reachable commits between the trusted base and prepared HEAD, including intermediate content; diff is presentation only. `git archive` is not an exact raw-tree transfer because repository export attributes can omit or transform files. Push the explicit prepared object id to `refs/heads/kora/<graph>/<run_id>/<node_path>/<n>` at the configured remote, with create-only compare-and-set semantics. A candidate branch name or remote cannot redirect it. Remote equality with the prepared object is success; another value is `publication_conflict` (`artifact_invalid`) and is never force-overwritten. Candidate commits or explicitly granted code-host credentials cannot redirect this platform operation, but candidate code given its own publication credential can make independent side effects (FR-6.1b).

**Idempotency and failure.** Store the `PreparedPublication` and native bytes before accepting late authorization. Concurrent `finalize` calls for an operation serialize; identical repetitions return one `VcsRef`, different bindings fail. After a lost response, query the trusted destination with safe provider configuration: the same revision is success, authoritative absence permits retry of the same prepared revision only before the inherited deadline, after earlier dispatchers and in-flight calls are closed and while the attempt is not retired, unexpected content is conflict, and unreachable is still unknown (`infrastructure`, retryable). Do not recreate commits, repeat collection against mutable candidate files, or claim success from a missing response. Record each reconciliation as `vcs.publish_repeated`. Lost sealed/prepared state fails `infrastructure` and leaves any existing remote branch discoverable under its deterministic name; another attempt has another branch and never adopts it. There is no database outbox: the persisted attempt and the runtime's private phase checkpoint retain the operation identity. Lease/result fencing still governs API uploads; it cannot revoke an external push already in flight. A point-in-time absence while an older call remains possible is not a settled outcome. Unknown kora publication retains the execution-recovery barrier and reservations; after retirement or deadline expiry only recorded results/read-only reconciliation can be returned, with no new push or change-request creation (ADR-0017/ADR-0022). A reconciled operation outcome after expiry is historical evidence, not current-result/success authority.

Target identity validation occurs at API/scheduler source-result acceptance, after physical closure, not before every external effect. A rejected exported source result cannot become current, but already produced branches and arbitrary candidate/MCP effects are not rolled back. All target-source output reports, including the final session/result artifact, retain immutable full-payload identity for exact read-only replay; selected digest fields alone cannot establish equality. See [data-model section 12](data-model.md#12-immutable-run-target-identity-adr-0019).

### Executor and slice-1 git provider (issue #159)

`kora.executor` runs the plans of this section; `kora.plugins.vcs.git` (entry point `vcs.git`) is the slice-1 provider. Neither keeps state between calls: every result is a frozen model that the runner stores in its checkpoint unchanged.

| entry point | result |
|---|---|
| `prepare_workspace(plan, request, areas, protected_paths, publishing, ctx)` | `CheckoutResult`: the `commit`, `branch` and `changed_paths` captures, the base (the `VcsRef`'s own base, otherwise the commit) and the plan digest |
| `export(plan, workspace, areas, ctx)` | `ExportResult`: the sealed format and the candidate-side export directory `.kora/export` |
| `seal(exported, workspace, areas, ctx)` / `verify_sealed(sealed, areas)` | `PublicationInput`; `verify_sealed` recomputes the digest of input a worker-host executor received |
| `prepare_and_inspect(plan, sealed, workspace, request, pinned, areas, secrets, protected_paths, sink, ctx, persisted)` | `PreparedResult`: the `PreparedPublication`, the `PreparedRevision` handed to `sink` before inspection, the `ScanVerdict`, the pinned binding and the destination pin |
| `publish(prepared, plan, authorization, grant, permit, ctx, recorded)` | `PublishOutcome`: `pushed`, `equal`, `conflict` or `unknown`, the `VcsRef` when settled and the pushing principal |
| `reconcile(prepared, ctx)` | `RemoteState`: `absent`, `equal`, `different` or `unreachable` |

**Steps.** `argv[0]` names one of the plan's `required_tools` and is replaced by the absolute path resolved at start-up; the environment is exactly the step's `env` after substitution. Placeholders are the seven names above, substituted in argv, env values and stdin; other brace text stays literal, a placeholder without a value in its phase is `config_error`, and export steps may not name `{control_root}`, `{publication_root}` or `{input_location}`. Checkout steps run in the candidate or control area, export steps in the candidate area, other steps in the control or publication area; each entry point gets a fresh control directory that it removes on every path, and `PlanFile`s are created there with descriptor-relative, no-follow, exclusive opens. Every step timeout is clipped with `useful_phase_bound_ns`; a due interruption or a zero bound starts nothing and fails `timeout`. Stdout is kept only up to its capture's bound and stderr up to 64 KiB, both drained to the end; a capture over its bound or malformed is `artifact_invalid`. A step that exits non-zero fails with its `failure_class`, or by phase when null (`infrastructure` for checkout, prepare and publish, `artifact_invalid` for export and inspect). A step is ended with SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after a 2 s grace; the helpers terminate their own Git children on SIGTERM, and those children inherit the step's stderr so a survivor keeps it open; the launcher does not reap a child until it has decided its outcome, observes exit through a pidfd on Linux and a kqueue `NOTE_EXIT` filter on macOS, so a signal never reaches a reused pid, and reads `wait4` resource usage. When a pipe stays open 5 s after the child exits, a descendant still holds it: the step is `unknown` (`step_outcome_unknown`, `infrastructure`) and its area is left for the runner to release. Children stay in the caller's process group.

**Sealed format `kora.git.v1`.** The candidate-side export writes `head.json` (`{"head", "bundle"}`), `head.bundle` when HEAD differs from the checkout commit, and `manifest.jsonl` (`{"path", "gitlink"}` per tracked or untracked non-ignored path). Sealing walks every path component with `openat(O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY)` from the candidate root, copies regular files and symlink targets as data, refuses special files, files on another device, absolute, traversing or control-character paths, `.git`/`.kora` components and their case, HFS-ignorable, trailing-dot or space, stream and 8.3 equivalents, duplicates and case-folded collisions (`artifact_invalid`), and writes `head.json`, `head.bundle`, `tree.jsonl` (path, mode, size, SHA-256, chunk, offset, gitlink object) and `tree.<k>.bin` chunks of at most 1 GiB into a fresh publication directory. The digest is SHA-256 over the file digests in that order. Bounds: bundle 512 MiB, manifest 64 MiB and 50,000 records, 2 GiB of tree bytes, 256 MiB per file.

**Prepare and inspect.** Prepare runs in a fresh `prepared-*` directory of the publication area. After the `commit` capture, the executor reads the prepared commit's raw bytes through the provider's `InspectionSource`, computes `prepared_digest` over the commit, its bytes, the base, the input digest and the plan digest, hands the `PreparedRevision` to `sink.keep`, and writes the digest to the file `prepared_digest` in that directory, which the decoder copies into its `InspectionManifest`. A persisted revision is reused without running prepare again; a changed plan or input digest is `workload_conflict` (`config_error`). The scanner reads the content index and every record through the `InspectionSource`, requires the index locations to equal the source's newly reachable commits and blobs, checks each size and digest, and scans contents, the paths of the index and of `changed_paths`, and revision metadata for the resolved secret values (substring searches) and fixed key patterns (one bounded regular expression) over 1 MiB chunks with an overlap, searching chunks of 64 KiB or more off the event loop; more than 256 values are refused before scanning (`config_error`). Bounds: 100,000 records, 2 GiB scanned, 256 MiB per record, 10 minutes clipped by the deadline. Protected paths compile once before any effect (`GlobInvalid` is `config_error`) and are checked over the deduplicated `changed_paths` in a worker thread with a 2^34 budget, a 2^24 cap per path and cancellation between 2^28 slices; exhaustion fails closed as `protected_path_modified`, and a due interruption sets the same cancellation flag and fails `timeout`. Sealing is likewise bounded by the deadline and stops at its next block when it passes. A `prepared-*` directory whose preparation fails or is cancelled is removed, unless a step was left `unknown`. A prepared revision equal to the base with no changed path is `artifact_invalid`.

**Publication.** `publish` refuses with `publication_authorization_mismatch` (`config_error`), before any step, unless the authorization's attempt, execution domain, operation, both digests, binding tuple and principal identity equal the pinned `PreparedResult`, its dispatcher and domain equal the grant's, its mode is `local_credential_free` with no value, and the deadline is not due. Without a `PublishPermit` for the operation it returns the recorded outcome or `unknown` and pushes nothing. The destination's pin and digest are rechecked before and after the push step; a change after the push makes the outcome `unknown`. A push that exits non-zero is settled by a head read: the prepared object is `equal`, another value `conflict`, anything else `unknown`; a killed, signalled or timed-out push is `unknown`. `reconcile` checks only the destination pin and digest and reads the head; it applies no deadline, binding or dispatcher check and is never clipped. `destination_digest` covers the provider name, the normalised remote path, the repository, the branch, the base and the transport settings `{"transport": "file", "push": "create_only"}`, not the binding revision.

**The git provider.** Configuration maps each logical repository to a remote path, one binding (identifier, revision, `local_credential_free` only), and a `local_service` principal whose `stable_id` differs from the binding, the path, the OS user and the commit identity; it also names the commit identity and the tool search path. `destination` compares the remote's current identity with the pin taken at start-up and refuses a substituted repository. Start-up resolves `git` (and `prlimit` on Linux) from that path, refuses Git older than 2.43.0, which first has `attr.tree` ([research](../research/git-version-2026-10-25.md)), checks every remote is an existing bare repository and pins its `(st_dev, st_ino)`. `acquire_credential` refuses `api` and any mismatch of repository, binding, revision or mode as non-retryable `KoraProtocolError`s and otherwise builds the no-secret result from configuration. Requests carry full hexadecimal object ids or full refs, branches pass the `check-ref-format` rules without a leading `-`, a publication branch starts with `kora/` and the commit message is `kora: <node_path> #<n>`; argv puts `--end-of-options` before request operands. Git configuration reaches every Git process, including the helpers' children, through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_<n>` and `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_<n>`, with `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, a private `HOME` and `PATH` limited to Git's directory; trusted phases also set an empty private hooks path, no fsmonitor, file-only protocols, no credential helper, no automatic or detached maintenance, the push step carries only the client options, because a local `receive-pack` inherits the push's environment and must run the destination's own hooks and configuration; publisher phases add `attr.tree` set to the empty tree, `core.attributesFile=/dev/null`, `core.protectHFS`, `core.protectNTFS`, object checks on transfer, two pack threads and bounded pack windows. On Linux publisher helpers and Git children run under `prlimit --as=2147483648 --fsize=1073741824`. Three standard-library helpers run as plan steps with the interpreter in isolated mode: `export.py` in the candidate domain, `tree_build.py` (bundle checks against the checkout commit, strict import, descent check, and the final tree through `hash-object --no-filters --stdin-paths` and `update-index --index-info` on a separate index, with one extra commit when the tree differs) and `decoder.py` (the content index from `rev-list --objects` and one `diff-tree --stdin -m --root -r --raw --no-renames` process over every new commit and each of its parents, LFS refusal, `changed_paths`). Prepare fetches the checkout commit with its full history, so a push succeeds even when that commit is no longer reachable from a destination ref. A `VcsRef` checkout verifies both its commit and its base on the remote (`config_error` when either is absent). `head_sha` runs `ls-remote --exit-code` with a 30 s bound and 64 KiB of output: a missing ref is the non-retryable `vcs_ref_missing` of class `artifact_invalid`, an unreachable or missing repository is retryable, a substituted repository is `vcs_destination_changed`.

The legs these entry points leave to the runner, the worker, Docker and a code host are listed with their triggers in the [implementation plan](../../docs/implementation-plan.md#7-named-contracts-by-work-item).

## 5. Approval channel (FR-7.1, FR-7.2a)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ApprovalLookupPage:
    request_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    next_cursor: str | None

class ApprovalChannel(Protocol):
    name: str
    def capabilities(self) -> ApprovalCapabilities: ...
    async def request(self, req: ApprovalRequestDto) -> str: ...
    async def lookup(self, approval_request_id: str, cursor: str | None) -> ApprovalLookupPage: ...
    async def poll(self, channel_request_id: str) -> ApprovalResponseDto | None: ...
    async def feedback(self, channel_request_id: str) -> list[FeedbackComment]: ...
    async def notify(self, channel_request_id: str, event: Literal["invalidated", "expired", "cancelled", "resolved"]) -> None: ...
    async def cancel(self, channel_request_id: str) -> None: ...
```

Built-in: `cli` (the API itself is the channel; `poll` reads the row) and `vcs` (publishes the `ReviewBundle` as a change-request comment with facts and narrative apart, maps review states to decisions, returns the head SHA and the approver's login and permission level with every response). The channel never decides validity: the scheduler compares SHA and digests and applies the DD-35 rules (approver is not the author and not kora's bot; on the namespace's `approver_allowlist` when set; "merged" counts only when the channel configuration sets `accept_merged_as_approval`).

Approval views render artifact text under the [VcsRef inert-presentation rule](artifacts/README.md#7-summary-and-availability), preserving facts/narrative boundaries and renderer-owned structure. Presentation encoding never changes the bound payload, revision or digests used by the scheduler.

A push to the candidate branch during review is not the channel's business either: the scheduler observes the head of the bound `VcsRef`'s branch through the VCS protocol's `head_sha`, moves the request to `invalidated` and fails the node with class `candidate_moved` (scheduler.md section 5); the channel learns of it through `notify("invalidated")`, and a response it reports afterwards is recorded and ignored.

`ApprovalCapabilities` retains `push_notifications`, `carries_identity` and `observes_head_sha`. `ApprovalRequestDto` binds the stable approval id, saved form/context, immutable SHA/digests and the saved versioned `run_cost` of [data-model section 7.4](data-model.md#74-immutable-approval-cost). Every channel presents that cost next to the form, including nullable USD, known subtotal, completeness and billing state, and reuses it unchanged on delivery/retry. The response form cannot replace accounting data. Response DTOs carry payload, the reviewer's `PrincipalRef` and permission, timestamp and observed SHA/digests; the scheduler, not the channel, derives the author and automation classification from the recorded publication principals and the binding's automation-principal set (section 4, ADR-0023), so a token replacement or bot change cannot let earlier automation or the author approve; feedback retains author/association/body and the existing collaborator filter. Only shared scheduler/API transitions decide validity.

`lookup` is strictly read-only on the configured original channel resource: enumerate every request/comment carrying the approval marker, including duplicates, through bounded pages and resumable cursor. Default at most 100 ids per page, within installation byte limits. Persist progress between calls and repeat scans with backoff to find late creation. Empty/complete pagination means none observed in that scan, not proof an earlier authorized write cannot appear. A provider with affirmative operation-closure evidence may expose that evidence through its delivery reconciliation metadata; lookup absence cannot invent it. `request` reconciles the same marker before creation while its local intent remains open; search-before-create does not establish generic exactly-once publication.

| request/gate state | durable desired state and channel delivery |
|---|---|
| Current pending request/open wait | `open`: reconcile/create under its stable marker and poll |
| Approved awaiting fresh VCS head verification | `closed`: no new create/poll authorization, `notify(resolved)` and transport `cancel`; local gate still waits |
| Approved and gate succeeded | Remains closed; canonical status shows approved revision and completed gate |
| Approved and gate failed/superseded | Remains closed; canonical status separates immutable approval from failed/current gate |
| Rejected | `closed`: notify resolved and cancel transport |
| Failed (`approve_when` could not be evaluated, [ir.md section 7.7](ir.md#77-evaluation-errors)) | `closed`: `notify(resolved)` linking the canonical status, which shows `failed`, and cancel transport; never shown as approved |
| Invalidated, expired, cancelled or historical owner | `closed`: corresponding terminal notification and transport cancel; never create during lookup |

`cancel` closes the channel interaction, not the immutable approval audit decision. If native code-host reviews cannot be disabled, mark the kora interaction closed; subsequent responses have no local authority. `notify(resolved)` says the request is no longer actionable and links canonical status; it never claims pending head verification succeeded. All terminal messages remain compatible with closure, even if remote delivery is delayed, duplicated or reordered. The API, not channel text, is authoritative.

Persist dispatch authorization before calling. A terminal revision prevents new create authorizations/retries; a preterminal authorized invocation may still send after cancellation. Record learned ids from all late results on the original approval even if their revision is obsolete, then close each id. Do not discard an effect result by applying the stale-read rule. Retain compact terminal reconciliation identity/progress without form/context payload holds, runtime reservations or an abandonment shortcut. Original-resource authorization failures are visible, not retargeted. The exact postcommit lifecycle is [data-model section 14.3](data-model.md#143-approval-delivery-intent-and-uncertain-effects).

**Requested reviewers** ([ADR-0025](../../docs/decisions/0025-notifications.md)). After opening the change request, the `vcs` channel calls `request_reviewers` with the channel configuration's `request_reviewers` list: provider-specific opaque identifiers, such as a GitHub login or team slug, that the provider validates and reports as `requested` or `rejected`. An empty list sends no request and leaves the host's own routing unchanged. A provider declaring `reviewer_requests: false`, or a failed request, writes `vcs.reviewers_request_failed` and never fails the node; success writes `vcs.reviewers_requested`. The call uses a code-host `api` credential. Requested reviewers grant nothing: ADR-0008, the approver allowlist and the principal checks decide what counts.

## 6. Secrets (FR-10.1)

```python
class SecretsProvider(Protocol):
    name: str
    config_schema: JsonObject       # includes `namespace_prefixes`: namespace -> allowed reference prefixes
    async def resolve(self, reference: str, ctx: ProtocolContext) -> SecretValue: ...   # secret://<provider>/<name>; refuses references outside ctx.namespace's prefixes
    async def list_names(self, prefix: str) -> list[str]: ...                           # for validation of aliases, never values
```

Secret references are **bound to the namespace** (current contract [ADR-0011](../../docs/decisions/0011-namespaces-and-personal-tokens.md)): the provider configuration maps each namespace to the prefixes it may reference; `kora validate` enforces it (`IR007`) and `resolve` enforces it again at submission time. Resolution happens inside the runtime adapter at submission time, after the nonsecret request has been durably sealed (ADR-0018); the saved request contains references only and current namespace authorization is checked again. Credential injection is outside semantic hashing and never serialized back into history; `SecretValue` registers itself with the redaction filter that every captured stream, error detail and VCS result passes through (FR-10.2). Built-in: `env`, `file`, and `scenario`, which serves only the values a scenario declares and is the only secrets provider a scenario run loads. What secret handling guarantees and does not guarantee in MVP is stated in [ADR-0009](../../docs/decisions/0009-target-repository-trust-boundary.md) (amended 2026-10-18): an agent node's secrets fail closed without enforced egress, an MCP server's secrets stay outside the agent on runtimes declaring `isolates_tool_secrets`, the model API key stays in the harness environment until a credential proxy exists, and redaction covers literal values only.

The `env` provider never resolves or lists a name beginning with `KORA_`, including `KORA_TOKEN` and `KORA_MCP_TOKEN`, or a configured name holding the worker's own credential. This exclusion applies even when namespace prefixes otherwise permit the reference. Refusals are content-free and do not reveal whether a forbidden variable exists; listing omits forbidden names and returns no values.

**The `env` provider (issue #192).** A reference is `secret://env/<NAME>` with `<NAME>` matching `[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]{0,127}`, and a namespace's prefixes are prefixes of `<NAME>`, the form `IR007` applies. The configuration has `namespace_prefixes`, a map from namespace id to its prefixes, and `excluded_names`, the worker's own credential names, which the composition root builds from the worker's configuration. Construction refuses an empty prefix, a prefix that overlaps a reserved family in either direction (`KORA_`, `AWS_`, `GITHUB_`, `GH_`, `SSH_`, `ANTHROPIC_`, `OPENAI_`, `CLAUDE_`, `CODEX_`, `GOOGLE_`, `AZURE_`; a namespace that needs such a credential gets it under another name set by the operator) and a prefix of an excluded name. The provider reads the live process environment at `resolve` time and never caches a value. `resolve` refuses, in this order, so a caller learns nothing about a variable it may not read: `secret_reference_invalid` for the syntax; `secret_reference_not_allowed` for a name in a reserved family (`KORA_` included), an excluded name or a name outside `ctx.namespace_id`'s prefixes, checked again at resolve and list time even when the configuration was not validated; `secret_resolution_unsealed` while `ctx.request_digest` is unset; `secret_admission_missing` without `ctx.secret_admission`; `secret_missing` for an unset variable; and `secret_admission_refused` for every `SecretAdmissionError`, whatever its reason. Each refusal is a non-retryable `KoraProtocolError` with a fixed message, empty details and no exception chain. `list_names(prefix)` answers only a prefix that starts with a configured prefix, with sorted names, at most 1,000, never values, reserved-family or excluded names. Its signature has no context, so one namespace's validation can see another namespace's names under configured prefixes; that disclosure of names is accepted. It prevents ordinary configured secret resolution from forwarding control-plane credentials, not same-user process or filesystem access. [Issue #192](https://github.com/realkarych/kora/issues/192) implements this #141 contract through `test_env_secrets_never_resolve_kora_variables`, including resolution and listing, allowed-prefix overlap and present/absent forbidden names. Its existing provider conformance and observability obligations remain required.

**`SecretValue` (issue #134).** It is a plain slotted class in `kora.protocols` with a private attribute, not a dataclass or Pydantic model.

- **No rendering path reveals it:** `repr`, `str`, `__format__` and f-strings, logging, pickling, `copy`, `dataclasses.asdict` and Pydantic `model_dump`; serialization raises. Equality is identity, and there is no value hash.
- **`reveal()`** is the only reader, used only by trusted code that builds a process environment or credential input: the harness launcher, the command and MCP server environments, the checkout and publication steps, and the VCS provider's acquire and publish path. A static test enforces that `reveal()` appears only in `kora.runner`, `kora.executor` and the `plugins.runtime`, `plugins.harness`, `plugins.vcs` and `plugins.secrets` families, and that the private attribute is read nowhere outside the class and the transfer codec (`kora.protocols.secret_value`); `kora.conformance.secrets` and `tests/` are exempt, because they check the value against what they admitted.
- **Crossing a process boundary.** It crosses the worker credential route and the runtime control channel only as a typed transfer field that hides its input in validation errors and is never logged, persisted or digested. The encoded field type has the same rendering rules as `SecretValue`: no `repr`, `str`, logging or `model_dump` path reveals it. The transfer codec in `kora.protocols` is the only other sanctioned access path, and its serialization of the field into a response or a control-channel frame is the one sanctioned exception to the rendering rules; `kora.models` declares only the encoded field, never `SecretValue` or a plain string; `kora.api` and `kora.worker` hold a `SecretValue` only to hand it to the codec or to a trusted consumer, and never reach the plain value. Every receiving process decodes the field through the codec on receipt (the worker on the issuance response, the runner on the control channel), so the value is registered before any capture. The issuance response is exempt from idempotency replay storage ([ADR-0023](../../docs/decisions/0023-code-host-identity-and-purpose-scoped-credentials.md)), and, under issue #134, kept out of access and debug logs, client response logging and HTTP caching (`Cache-Control: no-store`).
- **Registration.** Every process that constructs or deserializes the value (API issuance, worker, runner, publication helper) registers it with the redaction filter of `kora.observability` and gets a bounded registration scope back; `SecretValue` hands the plain value to the filter itself. When registration fails, the value cannot be revealed and secret admission fails, content-free. A scope is released only after its dependent captured output has been sanitized: transcript collection, stderr drain, segment upload and the publication helper's output. In the API, the scope is released when the issuing request completes, so registrations never accumulate across issuances. A value constructed while decoding the transfer field registers into the attempt scope of the decoding process, never into the object's lifetime. That scope is released at every end of the attempt in that process (success, failure, cancellation, lease loss, adoption by another worker and shutdown), once its dependent output is sanitized. A live mask is never evicted silently; on a `finalize` retry, the earlier scope is released once that try's publication output has been sanitized. The filter belongs to work item 1, and registration lifetime and rotation evidence to work item 14.
- **Forms and admission (issue #204).** A value registers its literal and the bodies of its two JSON-escaped forms, `json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=True)` and `json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False)` without their quotes, each only when it differs from the literal; identical forms count once in a scope. Text that UTF-8 cannot encode, such as a lone surrogate, is refused before any form registers. A value that needs escaping therefore takes up to three of the registry's entries and bytes, which reduces the effective capacity for such values. Each form is at most 4 KiB of UTF-8, the registry's per-value bound (issue #192), so a value whose escaped form passes it is refused. Admission is all or nothing: when any form is refused, the forms already registered for that value are released before #148's content-free `SecretAdmissionError` propagates, and no `SecretValue` exists. The transfer field validates its own input (type, encodable text, a 16 KiB bound and admission) and raises one fixed `secret_transfer_invalid` error with no context and no exception chain, so validation errors read through `errors(include_input=False, include_context=False)` carry nothing of the value.

Secret registration lifetime and finite admission/matching limits follow [the logging content contract](observability.md#5-safe-content-and-trust-boundaries): outstanding captured output retains its required masks, and insufficient protection fails secret admission before exposure. An ordinary diagnostic-sink outage does not remove coverage or stop scheduling.

## 7. Artifact storage (FR-4.4)

The staged storage contract is selected under [#229](https://github.com/realkarych/kora/issues/229#issuecomment-6368221981), with [owner approval](https://github.com/realkarych/kora/issues/229#issuecomment-6368257832), and amends [ADR-0005](../../docs/decisions/0005-eleven-protocols-with-conformance-suites.md). It replaces direct content-addressed put with private staging, immutable reread, create-only promotion and attributable cleanup. These names and records are the provider boundary; there is no direct-put bypass. Core owns authorization, validation, metadata intents and the shared owner fence. Providers own byte effects, immutable references, creation attribution and actual effect/resource closure. Workers use only the HTTP API.

The digest in a ContentAddress is the complete stored payload identity, never the artifact type's selected-field approval digest. Raw ingress, a canonical prepared candidate and a truncated retained transcript may have different complete identities; none is silently substituted for another. The [API](api-and-agent-surface.md#worker-route-bodies-issue-139) owns permitted preparation, and [metadata retention](data-model.md#10-retention-dd-4) owns the physical-store/full-digest admission/deletion fence across aliases and namespaces. No database transaction, connection or run/scope lock spans storage I/O. Typed core generations never by themselves fence an already-issued provider operation.

### 7.1. References and outcomes

All persisted DTOs are closed, immutable and JSON-only, with required version 1 and explicit finite identifier/collection limits. Digests mean complete SHA-256 bytes, never approval digest_fields. Trusted core creates operation identities before dispatch and never reuses them with different arguments. References contain only the named fields; payload streams are separate closeable async iterators, never serialized into a DTO.

| Name | Fields and meaning |
|---|---|
| StorageIdentity | trusted resolved physical-store id; same deduplicated address space has the same id across aliases/namespaces/processes/restart; changing display alias or credentials cannot change it |
| ContentAddress | storage_identity, payload_digest; StorageRef remains the configured logical read reference, not the fence key |
| StageRef | storage_identity, stage_id; caller-generated unique stage_id persisted before first provider call; never a path or arbitrary object key and never reused |
| StageSpec | StageRef, original declared payload_digest, total_size, content_type; immutable ingress declaration across begin replay, distinct from provider-computed raw and prepared byte identities |
| PartSpec | index, byte_offset, size, digest, final; immutable key (stage_id,index); API supplies already checked declarations, provider independently enforces them |
| PartRef | StageRef, exact PartSpec, immutable provider part identity; no byte payload in DTO |
| PrefixRef | StageRef, next source PartSpec, captured byte count/digest, immutable private identity, source_complete=false; a content-agnostic bounded final source prefix; core currently uses it only for permitted transcript truncation |
| SealedStage | StageRef, ordered manifest identity, total_size, full digest, source_complete; fixes the exact complete byte sequence; no subsequent append or replacement |
| PreparedContentRef | StageRef, preparation operation/request identity, actual size/digest and immutable content identity; at most one prepared candidate per stage, aliasing the sealed raw bytes when identical |
| ContentPreparation | operation ref, StageRef, bounded immutable source-part/prefix identity, maximum output size, expected output digest when known; bytes supplied as a closeable stream separately from this JSON-only DTO |
| StorageOperationRef | storage_identity, operation_id, kind, request_digest; persisted intent before each mutation, no reuse for a new deletion generation |
| PromotionRequest | operation ref, PreparedContentRef (or unchanged SealedStage), ContentAddress, admitted content_generation; core admission marker is not a storage-side delete fence |
| PromotionResult | exact operation/request identity, ContentAddress, immutable object incarnation, created_object boolean; result stays the original outcome on replay, even if content is subsequently deleted |
| MutationObservation | pending, settled or evidence_unavailable after acknowledged compaction; settled contains the typed operation result or confirmed no-effect failure and closure evidence; pending can include known effect but never asserts closure |
| StageRetirement | StageRef, retirement operation identity, closed-to-mutations state, remaining effects/bytes or completed cleanup receipt |
| DeleteRequest / DeleteResult | exact operation ref, ContentAddress and core deletion generation; result distinguishes settled absence/deletion from unknown; no generation-only or absence-only closure |
| StorageLimits | finite positive configured private_bytes, stage_slots and deletion_slots; persistent accounting covers live, accepted-awaiting-cleanup, failed and unresolved stages plus bounded per-stage receipt slots; deletion_slots bounds concurrent or unacknowledged content-deletion records and issued effects independently of stages; startup refuses absent/unbounded limits; implementing issue selects supported ranges/defaults with evidence |
| StageReleaseRequest | StageRef, persisted_outcome_revision, mutation_set_digest, issuance_closed evidence reference; core has persisted all outcomes, closed future issuance and proved all earlier issuers unable to dispatch again; provider separately proves its issued effects/readers closed |
| DeleteReleaseRequest | exact deletion operation/request identity, persisted_outcome_revision and trusted-core issuance_closed evidence; no later invocation of this operation may be issued; provider independently proves every issued effect closed |
| StorageScanCursor / StoragePage | StorageIdentity, kind (stages or content), last immutable key; positive caller limit clipped to provider's finite declared page bound; compact identity/state/size entries and next cursor, no bodies or arbitrary provider metadata |

Identifiers use the existing protocol Identifier domain (1–256 printable non-space ASCII characters), counts and generations use strict integers from 0 through 2^63-1, booleans are strict, and digests are the full SHA-256 byte identity in the existing digest representation. Each serialized DTO fits the existing 16 KiB upload-metadata bound and depth-16 JSON boundary; no DTO contains payload bytes. Content types remain bounded by that envelope. Manifests and semantic receipt arrays obey the [bounded generic part layout](api-and-agent-surface.md#bounded-chunked-uploads-issue-224): at most 16 public parts, with fixed prefix/preparation/outcome slots. Storage conformance exercises contiguous writes, exact replay/conflict, empty/final boundaries and the incomplete transcript prefix; real API/metadata tests prove wire responses and durable progress. The fixed 16 MiB non-final width belongs to generic chunked API uploads, not segment chunks or single canonical VcsRef uploads, whose existing caps/envelopes remain unchanged. Every manifest still fits the DTO bound; no full-size PartRef array bypasses it. Provider-private paths, credentials and arbitrary backend error text do not enter references, logs or public errors.

### 7.2. Operations and lifecycle

| Operation | Semantics |
|---|---|
| identity() -> StorageIdentity | Resolve/validate stable physical identity from trusted configuration; refuse aliases whose shared/different identity cannot be established safely |
| begin_stage(operation, spec) -> StageRef | Idempotent exact replay; conflict on changed binding; creates only isolated staging, never a content address; a retired stage cannot reopen |
| write_part(operation, stage, spec, stream) -> PartRef | Bounded streaming; independently hash/count and enforce contiguous append; commit one immutable part only on exact match; exact replay returns original receipt, changed tuple/bytes is conflict; concurrent same-index writes cannot both commit |
| capture_prefix(operation, stage, source_spec, count, stream) -> PrefixRef | Trusted core selects the permitted source cut; capture at most count bytes of the next part, hash actual bytes, and never claim its full source size/digest was verified; exclusive bounded slot, immutable on success, exact replay/conflict as other mutations |
| seal_stage(operation, stage, manifest) -> SealedStage | Irreversibly fixes ordered parts and at most one terminal PrefixRef; verifies no missing/extra captured bytes and actual byte size/digest; for source_complete=true requires the concatenated raw size/digest to equal StageSpec original total_size/payload_digest before any transformation; records whether source is complete; no later part writes can change sealed bytes |
| read_stage(SealedStage or PreparedContentRef) -> closeable async byte stream | Repeated reads are the identical complete byte sequence named by that exact private immutable reference; core performs its required full hash/validation reread outside all DB resources, including after preparation; cancellation/close releases readers |
| prepare_content(request, stream) -> PreparedContentRef | Stream and hash one immutable publishable candidate without rewriting source parts; enforce declared output cap/expected digest; exact replay preserves candidate identity, conflicting transformation refuses; at most one preparation slot and admitted writer per stage |
| promote(request) -> PromotionResult | Create-only publication of the exact selected immutable publishable reference (PreparedContentRef, or unchanged complete SealedStage), never replace; atomic effect-to-creator binding survives lost response/process crash; exact replay yields original created attribution; a new content generation requires a new promotion operation and current stage ownership |
| observe(operation) -> MutationObservation | Read-only reconciliation from durable evidence; an absent record/object or transport error is not confirmed no-effect/closure |
| retire_stage(operation, stage) -> StageRetirement | Permanently closes mutation admission for that stage, settles or authoritatively fences all issued stage effects, deletes staging-only data, then returns completed; repeated cleanup never deletes shared content |
| get_range(content, offset, length) -> closeable async byte stream | Exact bounded range over the same immutable content reference; actual seek, core clipping and empty-EOF handling, typed failure and reader closure follow [bounded range reads](#bounded-storage-range-reads-issue-227) |
| get(content) -> closeable async byte stream; exists(address) -> content reference or None | Bounded content access; absence is observational and grants no owner/result/closure authority |
| delete_content(request) -> DeleteResult | Retention only after durable owner fence; idempotent same-operation outcome, unknown remains unknown; core retains fence until every issued call/attempt cannot execute later |
| release_stage(request) -> pending or released | Acknowledge persisted outcomes and closed issuance after retirement; provider independently verifies stage effects and readers closed before compacting receipts/tombstone. Replays after release cannot recreate stage data |
| release_delete(request) -> pending or released | Acknowledge a durably saved deletion outcome after closed issuance and actual provider effect closure; compact provider receipts and core intent only after their durable acknowledgment handshake; no replay can delete recreated content |
| scan(kind, cursor, limit) -> StoragePage | Bounded keyset inventory of stages or content, across restart, with stable immutable keys; a page has finite bytes and actual scan work; concurrent changes can require another sweep and never authorize deletion |

Provider operation replay binds all arguments and returns conflict before mutating mismatched targets. No caller retries a mutation with a fresh operation identity just because its response is unknown. Read and reconciliation calls have finite deadlines but expiration does not close issued effects. Core durably closes issuance before retirement; provider must also stop late effects of previously issued invocations. In-flight requests, background threads and backend requests count until actual completion/fencing, not until coroutine cancellation. Closing useful issuance prohibits begin/write/capture/seal/prepare/promote dispatch; observation, retirement and acknowledgment remain permitted control calls. Retirement can close admission while an earlier data mutation is pending, but completed retirement still requires its actual closure. These control calls do not create another payload writer or bypass charged unresolved state.

Every first request also checks cross-reference binding before effects: each reference resolves through provider-owned records in the receiving StorageIdentity; parts belong to the exact stage; the manifest/seal matches saved immutable parts; destination digest/size equal the exact selected publishable immutable reference; a prepared reference belongs to the same StageRef and saved immutable source/preparation record; an incomplete source seal cannot be promoted directly and requires the core-validated prepared candidate; operation kind matches the operation; and the saved StageSpec binding is unchanged. Constructed identifiers never become paths, URLs or backend keys. A foreign/invented seal or mismatched destination cannot publish even with a fresh operation id. Local cleanup refuses symlink/path substitution; trusted same-user limitations remain explicit.

Provider-private capacity is separate from the existing core worker reservation. Begin reserves a stage slot and the known peak private bytes before storage; write/prepare reserves additional actual temporary capacity before allocation. Accepted-but-not-cleaned data remains charged here after core releases reserved_size. One data mutation per stage is admitted at a time; concurrent contenders receive bounded busy/pending results before consuming their streams. The slot remains occupied across unknown effects, including late thread/backend writes. Exact/conflicting requests do not create additional permanent receipts: use one bounded receipt slot per semantic stage/part/seal/promotion/retirement operation, retaining any active dispatch and closure state; completed no-effect retries cannot build an unbounded attempt ledger. Saturation refuses before allocation, never evicts unsettled evidence. Fixed numeric configuration ranges, startup defaults and measured peak amplification are owner-approved in the separate implementation plan, not implied RSS guarantees.

Receipts and stage tombstones are retained while core may need replay, while effect closure is unknown or while a previously issued invocation can still execute. Cleanup/release may compact them only after core has persisted the outcome, permanently closed new issuance and provider has authoritative closure for all issued attempts. Stage ids are never reused. Forgetting evidence cannot permit a delayed begin/write/promote to recreate data. A concrete implementation must state how this condition is proved and measured; if it cannot prove it, the stage/fence remains unresolved with its required reservation. No timeout-only reclamation.

Retirement also closes new stage-reader admission. Existing readers hold explicit provider-owned read leases; completed physical cleanup requires all such leases closed, including cancelled readers and descriptors held by still-live processes. Unlink alone is not reclaimed disk space on POSIX. release_stage carries the core acknowledgment; its issuance_closed evidence is trusted-core closure evidence, not a worker-provided flag, a DB generation, a lease timeout or object absence. A core crash before recording an outcome preserves the receipt. A crash after recording it but before release retries that exact release. If an older core issuer or backend dispatch cannot be proved closed, the bounded charged stage/tombstone remains. Compaction is forbidden until late begin/write/promote invocations cannot materialize; no claim of unconditional bounded recovery time.

A local feasibility witness uses a private provider-owned root, canonical root identity shared by symlink/config aliases, immutable staging files and atomic no-replace installation. A uniquely owned prepared inode/link can remain through durable result persistence so a crash between installation and receipt cannot confuse created with existing; the content-address owner fence prevents deletion during reconciliation. Overlapping roots are refused unless established as the same dedup domain.
